  [~] Start scifi_scripts' foundations, up to (NOT including)

      IN PROGRESS 2026-07-21: core/{channel,asic,feb,detector}.py written
      (first real scifi_scripts code, on branch
      gmoretti-userfile-script-implementation, not yet committed).
      - Channel: wraps ASICs/Channels/{tthresh,tthresh_offset,mask,sipm,
        cml} by GLOBAL channel index, via ODBCommand/CommandMappable.
      - Asic: 0-7 flat ODB index, module = position//4 (spec-book-
        authority split), owns 32 Channel, enabled from ASICMask bit.
      - Feb: feb_index (local) + link_feb (global, for FEBCrate/FEBSlot),
        owns up to 8 Asic + its own LVPowerSupply (common/power_supply.py's
        DCDCPowerSupply, reused as-is) - LV channel name derived from
        FEBName ("US SM 1" -> "SMB_US_2-3"), per code_design.txt 2.6. No
        HV attribute (deliberate, same decision).
      - SciFiDetector: SciFiFibre/LinksFibre only, no Labor/auto-detect
        (code_design.txt 2.7); builds Febs by mirroring scifi-userfiles'
        get_scifi_febs() logic (same fields, same link_feb fix) but as
        real objects; owns the shared MutrigConfig command.
      Verified via a scratch script (mocked realistic ODB array data,
      not the dummy-value-1 stub): all 12 Febs built, link_feb values
      match the real confirmed ones (144-155), Feb-name -> LV-channel-name
      derivation correct, module split correct (asic 5 -> module 1),
      global channel indexing correct across Feb boundaries (feb0's last
      channel = 255, feb1's first = 256). Also added test1.py (repo
      convention, mirrors pixel_scripts' test1.py) - confirmed it reaches
      the same known odb_read_test() dummy-value wall as test3.py/test4.py,
      no earlier wiring bugs.

      FOLLOW-UP 2026-07-21: adding write-persistence to the mock exposed
      a real flaw in test_hv_power.py's own assertion (not in core/'s
      logic) - it assumed only channel 20 would round-trip correctly
      (because only that one had pre-seeded readback data under the OLD,
      read-only mock); with writes now genuinely persisting, every
      channel's own write is what its own readback sees, so all 6 should
      (and now do) succeed. Fixed the assertion to match the corrected,
      more accurate mock behavior.

      set_channel_field_all() (single-shot whole-array read/write on the
      flat ASICs/Channels/* arrays - mirrors asic_control.py's
      set_array(), but write_channel_field_bulk() allows different
      values at different indices in one write, not just one uniform
      value). New test_bulk_channel_write.py verifies all of this,
      including that write_channel_field_bulk() only touches the given
      indices and leaves the rest of the 512-element array untouched.
      These are the two prerequisites flagged earlier as needed before
      calibrations/ can be designed for real - both now done.

C) Calibrations
  [~] Implement calibrations up to (not including) PLL test - still
      waiting on Yfeng for whether pll_test has a future (section 1b)
      - possibly split: threshold-scan (user) / IV-scan (colleague)
      IN PROGRESS 2026-07-21: calibrations/calibration.py written -
      CalibrationResult enum + CalibrationBase (prepare/execute/finalise).
      Deliberately simpler than pixel_scripts' own CalibrationBase: no
      power_partition_done/loop_over_power_partitions() parallelisation
      machinery (that exists there for multiple independent
      PowerPartitions running the same sweep at different paces - SciFi
      has no such concept; a SciFi calibration acts on every active
      channel at once via today's bulk channel-field write instead).
      Does NOT yet wire a result_saver - .root/ODB/CDB still open,
      finalise() left as the hook point, deliberately not designed
      further until scan_thr needs it for real. Verified: imports
      cleanly against core.detector.
      ADDED 2026-07-21: calibrations/calibrationfactory.py -
      CalibrationFactory (get_calibration_to_perform_from_odb/
      check_shared_equipment/perform_calibration, mirrors pixel's shape)
      + an empty CalibrationExecute enum (nothing to dispatch to yet -
      scan_thr is next). check_shared_equipment() is the same TDK/
      DCDCCrates check already wired into pixel's ActionFactory today
      (docs/todo.txt section 2b) - applies to SciFi too, same reasoning.
      BUG FOUND AND FIXED while testing: get_calibration_to_perform_
      from_odb() called client.odb_get() unconditionally, not checking
      setup.is_odb_available first like every other ODB read in this
      codebase (and like pixel's own identical method, which I had open
      but didn't port faithfully) - would have crashed with
      client=None/dry-run. New test_calibration_factory.py caught it
      immediately. Fixed, re-verified: shared-equipment check
      passes/fails correctly (TDK fault correctly flagged), and
      perform_calibration() cleanly returns NOT_READY (not a crash) with
      no concrete calibration wired in yet.
      ADDED 2026-07-21: calibrations/scan_thr.py - ScanThr(CalibrationBase),
      SCAN MECHANICS ONLY (offset x threshold grid, noisy-channel masking,
      in-memory scan_data) - deliberately NOT threshold-finding/.root
      export/plotting, still separate later work. Needed 2 new
      SciFiDetector primitives: read_fccr() (Variables/FCCR, the
      per-channel rate) and prepare_switching()/restore_switching()
      (SwitchingFibre bypass control, incl. the real "Unser Bypass" ODB
      command name - not a typo, kept verbatim from scifi-userfiles).
      New test_scan_thr.py (1 offset x 2 thresholds, noisy-channel
      masking verified to persist across thresholds within an offset but
      reset at the next offset). FOUND AND FIXED a test-infrastructure
      issue while writing this (not a core/ bug): MutrigConfig's
      ODBBooleanCommand writes True then waits for the SAME path to read
      back non-True, simulating the real switching-PC frontend finishing
      the command - with no real frontend in the mock, every configure()
      call was hitting the full 30s max_wait_time (test took ~2 minutes
      for 4 configure() calls). Fixed test_mock_odb_data.py to
      auto-flip MutrigConfig back to False right after a True write,
      simulating instant completion - test now runs in ~0.04s, same
      correctness. Full test suite (7 files) re-verified passing after
      this shared-mock change.

      ADDED 2026-07-21: calibrations/find_thr.py - the
      0.5/1.5/2.5pe plateau-based threshold-finding algorithm. The

      test_threshold_finding.py: a direct check that the ported

      ScanThr(run_autofinding=True) confirming the NEW part (the real

      channel with real data gets exactly 3 rows (one per pe_level)
      across the right Feb, every all-zero channel across BOTH Febs is
      correctly skipped with grouped warnings. Full 8-file test suite
      re-verified passing.
      ADDED 2026-07-21: scripts/thr_scan.py - 4th PySequencer entry point
      (feb_power/lv_power/hv_power/thr_scan), thin bridge over
      calibrations/scan_thr.py's ScanThr, same own-client/try-finally-
      disconnect shape as the others. Deliberately named thr_scan.py, not

      scan_thr is now functionally complete end-to-end (scan mechanics +
      threshold-finding + a real PySequencer entry point).
      CLOSED 2026-07-21: CalibrationFactory's dispatch. Added
      CalibrationExecute.SCAN_THR = "ScanThr", perform_calibration() now

      CLOSED 2026-07-21: result-saving, the .root half at least (CDB
      stays delegated, not built here - per docs/code_design.txt section
      2.5's 5.9, confirmed requirement is "AND", not "instead of").
      calibrations/root_writer.py - ported from scifi-userfiles/scripts/
      scan_thr/root_writer.py: the pure array-building helpers

      FEBs/channels with real, non-skipped data).
      Wired into scripts/thr_scan.py: writes data/scan_thr/
      run<N>_<timestamp>.root every run (user's call - same location/
      gitignore convention as scifi-userfiles' own data/, added to
      .gitignore), regardless of run_autofinding (calibration_by_feb is
      just empty -> no _calibration trees, same as the original).
      New test_root_writer.py: writes a REAL .root file from a real
      (small) ScanThr run and reads it back with uproot to verify actual
      tree/branch contents (run_info values, per-channel rate values,
      the exactly-one-FEB-gets-a-calibration-tree shape) - not just that
      write_scan_root_file() returns True. Full 9-file suite re-verified
      passing.
      REDESIGNED 2026-07-21 (same day, user's own idea after comparing to
      pixel's utils/results.py): result-saving now mirrors pixel's
      ResultSaver architecture (generic base class + swappable backends
      + a Multiple container broadcasting to all of them), instead of
      the single end-of-run write_scan_root_file() call from before.
      New calibrations/result_saver.py:
        - ResultSaverRoot: writes the raw per-step data INCREMENTALLY
          (one uproot tree.extend() per scan step, via a new
          append_step_result()) instead of holding everything in memory
          and writing once at the very end - a crash mid-scan no longer
          loses already-completed steps. Auto-incrementing filename
          (name_0.root, name_1.root...), same pattern as pixel's
          ResultSaverLocal.get_last_execution_number() - needs nothing
          from PySequencer/seq to pick a unique name. Calibration trees
          (from find_thr) still written once in save_final() - cheap,
          in-memory, no incremental benefit there.
        - ResultSaverODB: live/transient step status for real-time
          monitoring (the "live ODB" destination from
          code_design.txt 2.5's 5.9) - ODB path still a placeholder.
        - ResultSaverCDB: pure stub, mirrors pixel's own
          ResultSaverRunDB (also just __init__, no implementation) -
          CDB stays delegated, this just gives it a real slot to plug
          into later.
        - ResultSaverMultiple: holds several backends, broadcasts calls
          to all - mirrors pixel's own class of the same name.
      calibrations/scan_thr.py's ScanThr now owns a self.result_saver
      (constructed in __init__, has_root_result=True by default),
      prepare() calls record_initial_status(), execute() calls
      append_step_result() after every step, finalise() calls
      save_final(). scripts/thr_scan.py simplified accordingly - no
      longer does its own end-of-run root_writer.write_scan_root_file()
      call (would have written a SECOND, redundant .root file next to
      the one ScanThr's own result_saver already wrote incrementally) -
      just reports result_saver.get_root_file_path() afterwards.
      New test_result_saver.py - proves genuine incremental behaviour
      (not just "the final file looks right", which a buffer-then-write-
      once implementation would also pass): checks the on-disk tree's
      num_entries after EACH step (256 -> 512 -> 768), plus
      ResultSaverODB's live writes and ResultSaverMultiple broadcasting
      to all 3 backends. Updated test_scan_thr.py/test_find_thr.py/
      test_root_writer.py to pass save_root=False (they test other
      things, don't need file I/O side effects) and
      test_calibration_factory.py to redirect ScanThr's default output
      folder to a tempdir (perform_calibration() has no way to pass
      save_root=False through its own no-kwargs dispatch) - all so the
      test suite never litters the repo's real data/ directory. Full
      11-file suite re-verified passing, confirmed no stray files left
      anywhere in the working tree.
      NOT done yet: CDB (still delegated), scifi_scripts/globals/
      (get_calibration_to_perform_from_odb()'s ODB path and
      ResultSaverODB's odb_path are both placeholders, not yet real
      confirmed SciFi operation paths), reading ScanThr's actual scan
      parameters from ODB instead of constructor defaults (only relevant
      when dispatched via CalibrationFactory).

      POWER SCRIPTS FULLY AUDITED AND CLOSED 2026-07-21 (user design
      goal: feb_power/lv_power/hv_power read+check EVERY real ODB field

         ODB array is 32 channels but only 24 are real (8 provisioned
         spares, exact indices unconfirmed) - HV group summaries use
         live counts only ("N enabled, N on"), no fixed denominator.
         custom_page/custom_page.txt, custom_page/custom_page_spec.txt, and custom_page/custom_page_mockup.html
         all updated - mockup has real working expand/collapse and
         group-level select-all, verified integrity after the rewrite.
         ADDED 2026-07-22, same day: DCDC crate Sel.+batch RE-ADDED (3rd
         reversal on this one) - user's explicit call after seeing the
         grouping pattern, built 1:1 like Adjust/Demand (per-row inline
         buttons, unchanged, PLUS a new Sel.+batch "Reset selected"/
         "Clear Fault selected" pair). Also grouped US (DCDC_C_1..4) /
         DS (DCDC_C_5..8), same collapsible pattern as FEB/LV/HV.
         TWO BUGS FOUND AND FIXED in the mockup while building this:
           1. table-wide select-all wasn't updating each group's own
              select-all checkbox, only the row checkboxes underneath -
              fixed by having the table-wide handler also sync every
              .group-select checkbox for that table.
           2. expanding a group was also force-opening every row's own
              detail panel (FEB temps, LV sense/fault-flags) - root
              cause: both the summary row AND its detail row shared the
              generic "group-row" class, so the group-expand toggle
              applied to both indiscriminately. Fixed by excluding
              .detail-row from the group-expand selector, so a row's
              detail stays independent (click-to-open only), while
              collapsing a group still force-closes any detail left open
              inside it (no stale state).
         Both fixes documented in custom_page/custom_page.txt (3's geometric-
         grouping addendum) and custom_page/custom_page_spec.txt (3.4) as named
         "implementation pitfalls" so whoever builds the real page
         doesn't rediscover them the same way.

NOT PAUSED - separate research thread, IV scan vs pixel's VTX_iv_scan.msl
(2026-07-22): read both scan_vbr.py/iv_scan.py (scifi-userfiles) and
VTX_iv_scan.msl (pixel legacy) in full - comparison + a real, confirmed
SAFETY GAP written up in code_design.txt section 5.4 (extended, not
just the "worth reading" pointer that was there before). Short version:
pixel's MSL already scans per-power-partition with independent ranges,
a shifter-selectable mask, and a real over-current abort
(Max_Current_per_Chip x n_chips, checked live, cuts a partition's
Demand to 0 if exceeded) plus a pre-check that LV is on before sweeping
HV. scan_vbr.py/iv_scan.py has none of this - one shared range for
every channel, no selection, no early-stop, and NO over-current check
at all. Checked whether HV's frontend covers that gap itself the way it
does for voltage (mdev_hv4.cxx's real Voltage-limit clamp, already
documented) - it does NOT: HV/Settings/"Current limit" exists in the
live ODB (confirmed via the current dump, docs/odb_220726_121718.json)
but grep of mdev_hv4.cxx shows it's only ever stored/displayed, never
compared against the measured Current anywhere - so a script-level
over-current check would close a real gap, not duplicate an existing
one. NOT ACTED ON - same as scan_thr's own granularity question,
usefulness/priority needs Marius's input before investing in a redesign.

STANDING PRACTICE, ADOPTED 2026-07-22 (user instruction): whenever a
real safety/security gap turns up during this kind of research (like
the one above), write it up in whichever doc actually owns that topic
(code_design.txt for code/calibration-mechanics-level things,

without waiting to be told where - then add a short pointer here so
todo.txt stays the one place that indexes everything, even though the
full detail lives elsewhere. Applies going forward, not just to the one
instance above.

CHECKED 2026-07-22 (user asked for a real audit of the thr/vbr
implementations, not just trusting the narrative): compared both end to
end. Found and fixed one real bug in ScanVbr.prepare() - iv_step wasn't
validated, so iv_step=0 would hit a ZeroDivisionError mid-sweep in
execute() instead of failing cleanly (the original iv_scan.py has this
same latent gap, never validated there either - fixed here, not just
inherited silently). New covering test case, full suite re-verified
passing. Also confirmed and answered directly for the user: both
scripts currently act on EVERYTHING their discovery step finds, with no
selection mechanism - thr_run.py's ScanThr writes mask/tthresh/
tthresh_offset across the WHOLE flat 512-channel array (not filtered by
ASICMask/active status - mirrors asic_control.py's own set_array()
convention faithfully), vbr_run.py's ScanVbr sweeps every HV channel
matching the "SciFi CH" name pattern with no Enabled/ready check first.
Neither script reads its own scan parameters (threshold/offset/iv
ranges etc.) from ODB - those are always operator-chosen via
PySequencer's own param form. Only CalibrationFactory's own dispatch
(calib_run.py) reads anything from ODB automatically (which calibration
to run), and even there the scan parameters are just constructor
defaults, never read from ODB either - same "#TODO" gap pixel's own
CalibrationBase never closed.

CLARIFIED 2026-07-22 (user asked "si tratta dell'implementazione pagina
pyseq?"): confirmed this gap is UNRELATED to the custom control page.
The page's already-resolved design (custom_page.txt section 3) drives
thr_run.py/vbr_run.py DIRECTLY via PySequencer's normal Param/Value
mechanism - both already expose every scan parameter via their own
define_params(), no gap there. CalibrationFactory.perform_calibration()
is a separate, parallel path meant for a fully-automated/generic trigger
that only knows WHICH calibration to run (via CurrentCalibration in
ODB), not with what parameters - mirrors pixel_scripts' own
scripts/run_calibration.py (checked: same limitation there too, params
are never read from ODB, only the calibration selection is). SciFi
doesn't even have a run_calibration.py-equivalent entry point yet -
calib_run.py is only exercised by unit tests today. User confirmed
2026-07-22: deprioritized, not needed right now.

BUILT 2026-07-22: CalibrationExecute.SCAN_VBR = "ScanVbr" added to
calib_run.py's dispatch (mirrors SCAN_THR's own entry, same "constructor
defaults, not read from ODB" caveat). test_calibration_factory.py
extended with a real ScanVbr dispatch check (prepare/execute/finalise
through the factory, not just unit-level like test_vbr_scan.py/
test_vbr_find.py) - same scan_vbr.time.sleep patch-out as scan_thr's own
entry to avoid the real (slow) constructor defaults making the test run
for minutes. Full suite (10 files) re-verified passing.

BUILT 2026-07-22, same day (user: "aggiungi lo stesso controllo di
sovracorrente anche a thr"): ScanThr gets the LV equivalent of
scan_vbr's still-open HV over-current gap (calibrations_plan.txt 2.4) -
periodic checks of each Feb's real DCDCCrates "Over Current" hardware
fault flag (new Feb.check_lv_over_current() in detector/feb.py, no
UNVERIFIED tolerance guess needed, unlike check_lv_status()) during the
scan, masking every channel on a newly-faulting Feb and recording one
warning (deduped via a new self.__over_current_febs set, not re-warned
every step). Unlike noisy-channel masking (legitimately offset-scoped -
reset every offset), a hardware fault persists, so a new
__reapply_over_current_masks() re-applies it right after every offset's
mask reset. finalise() changed from overwriting self.warnings to
extending it, so run_autofinding=True doesn't drop these warnings.

TEST-INFRASTRUCTURE LIMITATION FOUND while writing the covering test
(not a thr_scan.py/detector bug): a first version of the test tried to
fault only one Feb and assert another Feb's channels were untouched -
failed with ALL channels masked. Root cause: common_scripts/common/
power_supply.py's PowerSupply.initialise_by_power_partition_name()
hardcodes _channel_number=0 whenever setup.is_odb_available is False
(dry-run/test mode), ignoring the real partition name entirely - so
EVERY commissioned Feb's DCDCPowerSupply collapses onto the same
DCDCCrates channel 0 in this mock environment (confirmed live: all 12
Febs report lv_channel=0). This is pre-existing, cross-repo (shared with
pixel_scripts) behaviour, already hit and documented once before in
test_lv_power.py's own comment ("stubbed to channel 0 in dry-run mode...
not something this repo controls") - not something to unilaterally fix
here. Rewrote test_scan_thr.py's over-current test to match this reality
(faulting the shared stub channel correctly masks/warns for every
commissioned Feb, not just one) instead of asserting something the mock
can't actually support. Real implication worth flagging: this mock
limitation means our test suite CANNOT currently verify per-Feb
LV-fault isolation (only that the mechanism fires and masks/warns
correctly in aggregate) - if that per-Feb distinction ever needs real
verification, the mock's channel-number stub would need to actually
route through find_odb_channel_number()'s own already-working
odb_read_test() fallback instead of hardcoding 0, in common_scripts
(cross-team change, flag to whoever owns that repo if raised). Full
11-file suite (test_scan_thr.py plus the 10 already listed above)
re-run and passing after the fix.

CLOSED 2026-07-22, same day (user: "over current possiamo implementarlo
ora? dove andrebbe fatto?"): the scan_vbr HV over-current gap flagged in
calibrations_plan.txt 2.4 is now built - ScifiHv gets read_current_
limits_all() (Settings/"Current limit"[], confirmed never enforced by
the HV frontend itself) and set_demand_channels() (per-channel-subset
Demand write); ScanVbr checks every step's Current against that limit
and cuts Demand to 0 for just the faulting channel (not a full-scan
abort, mirrors VTX_iv_scan.msl's own per-partition reaction), one warning
per channel, deduped and persistent for the rest of the sweep - same
shape as thr_scan's LV check above. Also fixed the identical warnings-
overwrite bug in ScanVbr.finalise() (run_vbr_finding=True was replacing
self.warnings instead of extending it, same class of bug just fixed in
ScanThr). New covering test in test_vbr_scan.py; had to seed a generous
Current limit in test_vbr_find.py too (its mocked breakdown current
legitimately hits ~1000uA, which would otherwise trip the new check
against the mock's dummy 1uA fallback). Full 11-file suite re-verified
passing. Full detail: calibrations_plan.txt 2.4.

FULL AUDIT + 3 FIXES 2026-07-22, same day (user: "fai un check finale su
vbr e thr... un check completo e approfondito" then "sistemali tutti"):
line-by-line audit of the whole thr/vbr calibration stack, cross-checked
against the original rate_scan.py. Fixed: (1) thr_run.py/vbr_run.py were
only printing calib.warnings when run_autofinding/run_vbr_finding=True -
over-current warnings collected during execute() were silently dropped
under default parameters, undermining the safety value of today's checks
- now always printed. (2) all 3 dispatch points (thr_run.py, vbr_run.py,
calib_run.py) skipped finalise() (hardware cleanup) whenever execute()
failed - real risk for ScanVbr via float-drift on the 60V cap - now
finalise() always runs, failure result still propagates. (3)
test_calibration_factory.py's own ScanVbr dispatch test was silently
degenerating (found via the audit) since it never seeded a Current
limit for its mocked HV channels - fixed, plus a new regression test for
fix (2). Full detail: calibrations_plan.txt 2.4 (same section, appended).
Full 11-file suite re-verified passing.

CORRECTED 2026-08-03 (user caught it, while auditing the real page/frontend
power-on conditions - see online/mhttpd/custom/SciFi_page/TODO.txt item 1):
hv_power.py's own copy of this gate was wrong, not just redundant - HV has
no real physical dependency on either TDK or DCDCCrates (mdev_hv4.cxx talks
to its own MSCB submaster directly, no DCDCCrates reference anywhere in

CLOSED 2026-07-22, same day (user decided all 3 remaining audit
observations at once: offset-reset masking confirmed correct as-is,
early-exit built, NaN edge case fixed): ScanThr/ScanVbr now abort early
when nothing is left to measure (every Feb LV-faulted / every HV channel
over-current-faulted) instead of scanning a dead range to completion:
new __all_febs_faulted()/__all_channels_faulted() checks. Had to guard
ScanThr's finalise() against a real KeyError risk this exposed
(run_threshold_finding() expects the full requested offset/threshold
range, not a truncated one) via a new self.__aborted_early flag - vbr_
find.py needed no equivalent guard, already handles partial curves. Also
fixed __check_over_current()'s NaN blind spot (abs(nan) > limit is
always False in Python, so a broken channel reading NaN would look
"fine" forever) - now treated as a fault. Found and fixed a real
pre-existing test bug while verifying: test_find_thr.py never seeded a
healthy LV baseline, previously harmless but the new early-exit turned
it into an immediate scan abort. test_scan_thr.py's over-current test
redesigned (monkeypatches Feb.check_lv_over_current() for true per-Feb
isolation, since the shared mock's own channel-0 collapse now
short-circuits the old approach via the early-exit). Full detail:
calibrations_plan.txt 2.4. Full 11-file suite re-verified passing.

  - POWER/READOUT CHAIN + MONITORING SWEEP, 2026-07-30: new doc
    docs/old/chain_and_monitoring.txt - planning only, companion to
    custom_page.txt (which still owns the page architecture). Started
    from the user's own discovery that LV/HV/FEB sit at the BOTTOM of a
    dependency ladder, not at the top. Contents: the chain has TWO
    branches (power TDKStaging -> Interlock -> PowerDistStaging(Slow
    Control) -> PowerDistStaging(SciFi&Tile) -> DCDCCrates Crate On ->
    per-Feb LV -> FEBPower, and readout ROSWB*/readout_scifi -> Run
    Parameters/"SciFi readout" -> SwitchingFibre counters); the
    interlock is READ-ONLY (a permissive, no ODB write path); the full
    monitoring surface, most of it previously undocumented (SFFE 26
    fields x 12 FEBs, SFLE, SFTS, FCCE/FCCP/FCCH/FCLS, SwitchingFibre
    SFSO/SFCN, cooling); staging->final parametrisation; three naming
    conventions for the same channel; two indexing pitfalls.
    THREE SAFETY GAPS written up there (chain_and_monitoring.txt
    section 5), per the standing practice above:
      1. the SciFi FEBs have NO over-temperature alarm - the existing
         Max/Average FEB Temperature alarms read LinksCentral/SCTS,
         SciFi's FEBs are on LinksFibre/SFTS (identical shape, so the
         alarm is a copy-and-change-the-path job). HV, TDK and
         TDKStaging have no alarms at all.
      2. someone else's bug, to report not fix: "FEB Crate 0
         Temperature" and "FEB Crate 1 Temperature" both read
         FEBCrates/Variables/SCFC[4] (the rest step by 20), so crate 1
         is not monitored.
      3. four defined-but-INACTIVE alarms that are ours: Min/Max SiPM
         Chiller Temp., Dew Point, TDiode. Reactivating is a shared-
         infrastructure change - ask first.
    Also reverses half of custom_page.txt 3.1's "DECLINED 2026-07-23"
    (DCDCCrates crate-level control): its factual premise was wrong,
    its safety premise stands and is now quantified per crate - see the
    PARTIALLY REOPENED note inserted there.
    Useful by-product: the Min/Max SiPM Chiller alarms give a REAL,
    in-service 15-25 C window - a verified constant available to
    replace an UNVERIFIED placeholder in code.

2026-07-31: SciFi_page real build started (online/mhttpd/custom/SciFi_page,
  on the skeleton the user scaffolded 2026-07-30) - Emergency/Watchdog/
  Required Programmes/Service Status/Alarms/Cooling cards live-wired to
  real ODB, then all 4 Power tabs (FEB/LV/HV/Supply) done, read-only,
  columns matched to custom_page_mockup_v3.html exactly (Sel./actions
  dropped everywhere - separate later step). FEB/LV detail content
  (SCFC+SFFE+datapath+firmware for FEB; Sense/Temperature/7 fault flags
  for LV) folded into a tooltip on the name cell instead of the mockup's
  expandable row.
  [!] REAL BUG FOUND, not yet fixed on the live system: topology.txt
      section 6 - LinksFibre/Settings/FEBName mislabels US SM0/2/3/4 (a
      4-entry cyclic swap), confirmed against the user's installation
      log + master wiring table, AND against the live mu3ebe ODB browser
      directly (still present as of 2026-07-31 10:58). FEBCrate/FEBSlot/
      FEBPower and everything else indexed by link_feb are correct; only
      the name is wrong. Also fixed in passing: SciFi_Emergency.js's
      scifiTurnOffLV() was matching LDO channels with a suffix regex
      (/_LDO$/) that never matched the real prefix names (LDO_US/LDO_DS) -
      the 2 LDO channels were silently skipped by every emergency LV
      shutoff. Both entries: see topology.txt section 6 for full evidence.
  [i] HARDCODED-VS-LIVE AUDIT, custom_page.txt section 6: user asked
      directly what the real page hardcodes vs. reads from ODB, prompted
      by the FEBName bug. Biggest hardcoded piece by far: SciFi_PowerHV.js's
      HV_BOARDS, the entire 32-cell board->module wiring map - does NOT
      self-correct if the physical HV wiring ever changes, unlike
      everything else on the page. Also DCDCCrates/Common/Enabled (single
      equipment-alive flag, always true) vs Settings/Enabled[] (per-crate
      array, real gate checked by mdev_pdcc.cxx - false means the frontend
      skips that crate entirely) are two different ODB keys with the same
      short name, easy to conflate when browsing the ODB tree by eye.

2026-08-06: common_scripts migration resumed (action/saver/power_partition
  analysis - pixel vs scifi_scripts vs what's already in common/). Before
  moving code, formalised PIXEL's bring-up sequence as data, same shape as
  chain_and_monitoring.txt 14's SciFi LV/HV/FEB sequences - new doc
  docs/code/pixel_bringup_sequence.txt, reconstructed from VTX_basic_
  functions.msl (ground truth, no such single list existed for pixel
  before). Planning only, no code touched.
  [!] TWO REAL GAPS FOUND, not fixed, comparing that reconstruction
      against the current pixel_scripts port (ActionPowerOn): (1) the
      power-distribution (PowerDistStaging/MPDC) turn-on step is entirely
      missing from ActionPowerOn.perform(), even though PowerPartition.
      turn_on_power_distribution() already exists; (2) no equivalent of
      the legacy ramp-to-target-HV loop (VTX_reach_target_HV_power_
      partition) exists anywhere in pixel_scripts - ActionPowerOn stops at
      a flat 10V. Full detail: pixel_bringup_sequence.txt "GAPS FOUND".
  Conclusion carried forward: the bring-up ENGINE (run list, stop at
  first failed step, dry-run, reverse for power-down) is subsystem-
  agnostic and belongs in common_scripts; the step lists themselves
  (this doc for pixel, chain_and_monitoring.txt 14 for SciFi) stay
  per-subsystem. Not yet decided: Step object shape, whether the two
  found gaps get fixed now or stay open.

  SAME DAY, follow-on: formalised the CALIBRATION startup procedure too
  (from PySequencer entry to calibration.execute() actually running,
  incl. where/whether a result saver gets involved) - new doc
  docs/code/calibration_startup_procedure.txt. Planning only, no code
  touched. Reconstructed pixel's real path (run_calibration.py ->
  CalibrationFactory) AND SciFi's real path (thr_run.py/vbr_run.py) -
  the two do NOT follow the same pattern, that divergence is the main
  finding.
  [!] TWO MORE REAL GAPS FOUND, not fixed: (3) pixel's run_calibration.py
      passes the PySequencer `seq` object itself as the ODB "client",
      while every SciFi entry script explicitly avoids this (own
      MidasClient) citing a documented interface difference - unverified
      whether pixel's actually breaks, but a real, checkable
      inconsistency; (4) no pixel calibration ever calls result_saver.
      save_final()/save_step_results() - only ActionBase.post_perform()
      does that, calibrations never get an equivalent call, so a
      calibration's .ndjson always ends without a closing result line.
  [i] ALSO FOUND: pixel has TWO duplicate copies of the shared-equipment
      check (ActionFactory + CalibrationFactory, byte-identical bodies)
      that never call common/services.py's own canonical
      check_shared_equipment() - SciFi already calls it directly.
      Cheapest fix in the whole investigation if/when code changes are
      wanted. Also: scifi_scripts' own calib_run.py CalibrationFactory
      is real but UNUSED - no PySequencer entry point calls it; thr_run.py/
      vbr_run.py bypass it entirely and build calibrations directly.
      Not decided: keep it, wire it up, or retire it as superseded.

  SAME DAY, PLAN CLOSED: docs/code/common_migration_plan.txt - final
  5-step order for the common_scripts migration (pixel first, scifi
  later): (1) unify Result enum + drop empty Message subclasses,
  (2) decouple ResultSaver from core.detector/core.daq via a
  det.get_status_summary() hook, (3) bring-up sequence engine applied
  to pixel (decide GAP 1/2 fix timing while building it),
  (4) calibration-startup common pieces applied to pixel - own
  MidasClient convention, drop the duplicate shared-equipment check,
  and OperationBase gets a run() method owning the whole lifecycle
  (opens the saver, drives perform/prepare-execute-finalise, ALWAYS
  closes the saver in a finally - this is what actually closes GAP 4,
  structurally rather than by patching one call site), (5) extract
  PowerGroup from PowerPartition. Save format and the full result_
  interpretation.py function list deliberately left to emerge while
  coding, not speced in advance. Next step after this doc: actual code,
  starting at (1).

  PLAN REVISED, two audit passes same day, still no code touched:
  reordered (PowerGroup extraction now before the bring-up engine, was
  after - the bring-up steps bind to PowerPartition/PowerGroup methods
  directly, doing it in the other order would touch the same call sites
  twice); added a 6th step (common_scripts/tests/ - confirmed pixel_
  scripts has no real test suite, only manual test1..6.py scripts, and
  common_scripts has none at all, despite becoming the shared
  foundation - reuses scifi_scripts' own proven mock-ODB test
  convention, no new framework needed).
  [!] TWO MORE REAL FINDINGS: append_step_result_and_check() is ALSO
      duplicated between ActionBase and CalibrationBase, and the two
      versions disagree on schema (nesting, selection-filtering, LVDS
      merge) - Action and Calibration step results are not even the
      same shape today; and ActionFactory.perform_action() has no
      try/finally around post_perform() either, so an exception mid-
      Action skips the save just like GAP 4 does for calibrations -
      wider version of the same problem. Also confirmed: ActionFactory
      is exactly as orphaned as scifi's calib_run.py CalibrationFactory
      (no run_action.py-equivalent PySequencer entry exists anywhere).
  Full detail, including the folder-name bug found and the compare_
  read_outputs() move: docs/code/common_migration_plan.txt.

  link_feb hook yet - separate task). Verified: py_compile, real-dump
  check confirms a real pixel FEB and a real scifi FEB both resolve
  correct crate/slot and read the same shared telemetry correctly.

- 2026-08-07: ODB vs CDB ROLES CLARIFIED (user, explicit): "ODB non
  deve essere un database delle run, ma un database per operazioni" -
  confirmed correct and written into calibration_process.txt. ODB =
  current/latest state only (Output/Voltage[]/Current[] gets
  overwritten by the next scan, no versioning - matches how the real
  VDAC_power_consumption precedent already behaves). CDB/RunDB = the

- 2026-08-07: SAVER.PY - 4 MORE REAL BUGS FOUND+FIXED (user pushed:
  "sicuro che questi due file siano a posto?" - re-checked against the
  REAL caller, OperationBase.run(), not just my own earlier design/
  tests). (1) OperationBase.__init__ never passed client= to
  ResultSaverMultiple - latent crash the day has_odb_result flips True;
  fixed, now passes det.client. (2) save_final() in the real flow gets
  the bare OperationResult enum, not a dict - was unconditionally
  writing Status="Done" regardless of actual outcome (a POWER_FAIL
  would've looked identical to success in ODB); fixed, Status now
  reflects the real enum value. (3) append_step_result was reading the
  OLD odb array back and appending to it - a fresh run on the same
  channel would silently inherit and grow the PREVIOUS run's data
  forever, violating the "ODB = current state only" rule agreed earlier
  today; fixed, now rebuilds the array from self.step_results (fresh
  per instance) every time, no ODB read-back. (4) added _normalize()
  for Enum values (the real Final_Result step contains a raw
  OperationResult member, not ODB-serializable as-is). Verified all 4
  with direct tests reproducing the real call shapes + full pixel
  test1-5.py suite - zero regressions. Full detail in calibration_
  process.txt's step 7, "THIRD PASS".

- 2026-08-07: REAL BLOCKER FOUND+FIXED - the Calibration page's Start
  button was silently writing NOTHING. Confirmed via MIDAS's own C++
  source (js_db_paste in mjsonrpc.cxx, behind modbset()): ODB writes via
  the page's RPC path require the key to ALREADY exist (db_find_key
  first, silent fail if missing) - unlike Python's odb_set(create_if_
  needed=True). Since /CosmicRun/Calibration/SciFi never existed, every
  page write silently failed. FIX: Docs/devolpment/code/
  seed_cosmicrun_scifi.py (new) - creates the whole tree from real data
  (real LinksFibre FEB list -> 24 real modules, real HV_BOARDS mapping
  for each module's HV channel), matching calibration_process.txt's
  agreed ODB structure exactly. Run against the LOCAL test experiment
  only (mhttpd -D) - mu3ebe untouched, per the hard rule. Verified the
  actual fix afterward: a page-style write on an existing key now
  succeeds where an identical write silently failed before seeding.
  Script is reusable - the user can run it against mu3ebe themselves
  later when ready to load this there for real.

- 2026-08-07: THR SCAN GRANULARITY DECIDED - ASIC level (user: "io
  farei asics", checked against real code before agreeing, not just
  taken as preference). Real evidence: both the legacy Scifi-monitor.js
  rate_scan() and today's thr_run.py write threshold GLOBALLY
  (tthresh[*], same value to every channel each sweep step - "whole-
  array bulk writes" per scripts_io_reference.txt), while thr_find.py's
  threshold-FINDING result is already per-channel by analysing each
  channel's own curve along that same shared sweep - no per-channel
  write differentiation was ever needed for that. So: parameter staging
  (threshold_start/stop/etc, IV scan's own per-module equivalent) goes
  to ASIC level, not channel - finer than needed, channel-level analysis
  already covered by thr_find.py regardless of sweep granularity.
  REAL IMPLICATION for the future thr_run.py rewrite: can't keep the
  blanket tthresh[*] write if ASIC-level ranges are wanted - needs the
  same per-item state machine already agreed for IV's own script-side
  granularity, scoped to ASIC. Full detail in calibration_open_items.txt
  section 2, "GRANULARITY DECIDED".

- 2026-08-07: CLARIFIED - Stop/Pause buttons need no future wiring.
  User asked if the new Stop/Pause buttons will need connecting to the
  future vbr_run.py/thr_run.py rewrite. Checked real sequencer.py
  source before answering: "Stop immediately"/"Pause script"/"Resume
  script" are handled generically at the PySequencer ENGINE level
  (sequencer.py:1597-1629, same polling loop as Start/Load new file),
  not inside any individual script - they already work for whatever
  script is loaded/running, no per-script code needed. So the buttons
  built today are functionally complete; the only real dependency is a
  real script actually running (roadmap step 4 itself), not extra
  integration work on top of it. Noted in calibration_process.txt
  ("STOP/PAUSE ARE ENGINE-LEVEL") and roadmap step 4.

- 2026-08-07: Power and Calibration sub-tabs (Supply/LV/FEB/HV power;
  IV scan/Thr scan/PLL test) now collapse when you click the already-
  active tab again, instead of just staying open with no way to close
  it - same "click to open, click again to close" idea as row-detail
  panels elsewhere on the page. One shared fix in initMtabs()
  (SciFi_Utils.js v21->v22) since both Power's and Calibration's
  sub-tabs use the exact same .mtabs/.mtab mechanism - no per-section
  code needed.
  FIRST VERSION DIDN'T WORK (user: "non funziona" -> "ho fatto refresh
  forte, non succede niente cliccando") - real root cause, not a cache
  issue: MIDAS's own core mhttpd.js has ITS OWN built-in .mtab click
  handler (line ~1122, attached via <body onload="mhttpd_init(...)">,
  i.e. on window load - LATER than SciFi_Utils.js's own initMtabs(),
  which runs during page parsing). That core handler always force-
  activates whatever tab was clicked (classList.toggle('active', t ===
  tab) - never actually toggles off), silently undoing the collapse
  logic on every click since both listeners fire on the same element.
  Fixed with evt.stopImmediatePropagation() inside the custom handler -
  reliable because it attaches first (DOM listeners on the same element
  fire in attachment order), so it wins the race and blocks mhttpd.js's
  own handler from ever running for these clicks. SciFi_Utils.js
  v22->v23. Worth remembering for ANY future custom click behavior on
  .mtab elements - the core page always adds its own generic handler on
  top, silently, on every custom page.

2026-08-08: user testing found a real bug - IV plot had no axes. Root
  cause: MIDAS's own mplot.js computes axis range via Math.min/max on
  the data array; with the (currently normal) empty-array case, that's
  Infinity/-Infinity, breaking axis drawing entirely. Fixed by setting
  an explicit default axis range (from ParametersUsed/iv_start,iv_end)
  when there's no live data yet. SciFi_Calibration.js v31->v32,
  verified live. Full detail in calibration_process.txt's "IV LIVE
  PLOT: MISSING AXES BUG FIXED" section.

2026-08-09: user pointed at Docs/devolpment/Mu3e-Note-0133-CDB_RDB.pdf
  (Urs Langenegger, v2.0) for the CDB save mechanism - read all 46
  pages. Found CDB actually has 3 distinct storage mechanisms: the real
  conditions DB (/cdb, heavyweight, needs a calAbs C++ class + schema +
  GT wiring), /detconfigs (opaque config bundles, no run versioning),
  and /detcal ("Detector Calibration Files") - arbitrary calibration
  output files keyed by (name, run) with IOV-style lookup, a simple
  already-working REST API (POST /detcal/upload, GET /detcal/:name,
  etc.), much lighter than the real conditions DB. /detcal looks like
  the real destination for scan_thr/scan_vbr's "must work by October"
  result-saving gap - not yet decided/implemented, just a grounding
  finding. Added to calibration_open_items.txt section 7's CDB/RunDB
  bullet.

  number, last completed run if DAQ stopped; one file per (name, run),

  User then narrowed the report: only the per-table "is not running"
  messages are wrong (Required Programs itself shows correctly green)
  - ruled out the ODB-corruption theory above for THIS symptom (wrong
  guess, corrected quickly once the user gave the extra data point that
  Required Programs and the table messages read the same ODB path but
  disagreed). Found the real cause: a genuine JS bug, systemic across
  5 files, not ODB-side at all. SciFi_PowerHV.js/PowerFEB.js/PowerLV.js/
  PowerSupply.js/Readout.js each do their own `programFirstFailed !==
  0` check on /Programs/<name>/First failed (a DWORD) - but mjsonrpc
  serialises DWORDs as hex STRINGS (confirmed earlier this session:
  "0x00000000" etc, never a bare JSON number), so a strict `!==`
  against the number 0 is ALWAYS true regardless of the real value -
  every one of these 5 tabs would show its "is not running" message
  permanently, independent of whether the program is actually up.
  Required Programs' own native .modbbox widget uses mhttpd's own
  formula evaluator (not raw JS strict equality), which is why it alone
  showed correctly. Fixed all 5 with parseInt(programFirstFailed, 16)
  !== 0, matching the same hex-parsing convention already used
  elsewhere in this codebase (FEBType/ASICMask/LinkStatus etc.).
  Versions: SciFi_PowerFEB.js v25->v26, SciFi_PowerLV.js v40->v41,
  SciFi_PowerHV.js v33->v34, SciFi_PowerSupply.js v37->v38,
  SciFi_Readout.js v9->v10. Verified live (all 5 files + HTML diffed
  against the real running local mhttpd, identical). Not yet confirmed
  against mu3ebe (would need the user's own check there) - but the bug
  is unconditional (wrong for ANY DWORD value that's a hex string, not
  data-dependent), so no live confirmation is really needed to trust
  the fix. FEB "not connected" report from the same conversation is
  UNRELATED to this fix (different ODB field, CrateControllerConnected,
  not First failed/programFirstFailed) - not yet investigated, separate
  open thread.

  - the shared-grid limitation above was a real gap, not accepted.
  Discussed 3 options (sequential per-item scanning; independent ranges

  real problem with the first version of the "shared increment" option
  (same number of steps, different range width -> different V/tick per

  instead. Implemented: VBR's iv_start/iv_end and Thr's threshold/offset
  ranges are now genuinely per-module/per-ASIC (VBR ramps every selected
  module at the same V/tick, narrower ones hold at their own end once
  reached; Thr's offset/threshold loops run over the union of every
  selected ASIC's own range, masking out whichever ASICs a given tick
  falls outside of). iv_step/settle_seconds (VBR) and settle_seconds/
  noisy_rate_threshold (Thr) stay shared - one settle time for the whole
  selection is a real hardware constraint, not a shortcut. VBR-finding
  params (fit_window_v/min_peak_prominence) also stay shared, since
  changing that means touching vbr_find.py's fit algorithm itself -

   (calStartThr() fires regardless of warnings, same pattern as every
   other page-side warning this session found not to be a hard gate).
   Python never checked Asic.is_enabled() at all, so a disabled ASIC
   selected by mistake would have gone straight into the real scan.
   Fixed: __load_selected_asics() now excludes a disabled ASIC (warn +
   skip, same pattern as the existing "no matching Feb/ASIC found"
   case) instead of silently including it. New dedicated test in
   test_scan_thr.py (toggles Asic.set_enabled(False) directly, since
   Feb/Asic objects are built once at SciFiDetector construction -
   an ODB-only ASICMask edit after that point has no effect on the
   already-built objects). Full 13-file suite green.
   Separately noticed, then FIXED, 2026-08-13 (user: "vanno sistemati"):
   find_asic()'s bounds check only validated the combined position_in_
   feb against the whole Feb's asic count, not asic_in_module against
   NASICS_PER_MODULE on its own - an out-of-range asic_in_module (e.g.
   7) could silently resolve to a DIFFERENT module's real ASIC instead
   of being rejected, if the combined position still landed in-range.
   Fixed: an explicit asic_in_module bounds check added before the
   position_in_feb computation. New test in test_detector_construction.
   py proves both the fix (asic_in_module=7 or -1 now correctly
   rejected) and that a valid call still resolves normally.

5b. DONE, 2026-08-13 (user: "vanno sistemati", same batch as above).
   Two more small gaps found earlier and flagged, now fixed:
   - common/power/group.py's PowerGroup.is_initialized() had a real
     pre-existing bug: BOTH its needs_power_distribution branches
     required hv_supply is not None, contradicting its own docstring
     ("Optional - a group can be LV-only"). Checked pixel's real
     config/detector.json before fixing (both its 2 real partitions
     already have hv_supply - zero behaviour change for pixel's actual
     setup) then fixed properly: lv_supply and hv_supply are each
     individually optional now (at least one must be present) -
     matches both of SciFi's own real shapes (LV-only per-Feb groups,
     HV-only per-side groups). New tests in test_bringup.py prove all
     12 real LV-only groups and all 4 real HV-only groups report
     initialized.
   - common/power/bringup.py's Step/BringUpSequence only printed
     per-step failure detail (server log only) - a caller's seq.msg()
     could only ever say generic "FAILED", not which step or why.
     Fixed: both classes gained a last_error attribute (set on every
     bring_up()/power_down(), combining the step name with its own
     detail e.g. "did not verify within 5s") - return type/signature
     unchanged (no existing caller broken), just an additional
     attribute to read after checking the result. lv_bringup_run.py/
     hv_bringup_run.py/feb_bringup_run.py updated to include it in
     their seq.msg() failure messages instead of pointing the operator
     at the server log. bringup.py's own __main__ self-test and the
     full 13-file scifi suite + pixel's test1-6.py all still green.

POWERGROUP ADOPTION (common+scifi), 2026-08-13
====================================================================
Started from "fai per feb" (build a real FEB bring-up sequence,
scripts/feb_power.py/hv_power.py/lv_power.py archived by the user as
obsolete). Investigating that surfaced common.power.feb_crate.py's own
build_feb_bringup_sequence() (per-FEB: crate power + link status) -
already generic, already used by pixel_scripts/actions/bringup_
declaration.py's build_feb_sequence(), with a comment saying "same
shape as scifi's own FEBs would use". That led to checking whether
SciFi should adopt common.power.group.py's PowerGroup the way pixel
does - user, explicit: "dobbaimo farlo ora. sistemare common+scifi".

FIT ANALYSIS (done before touching code): LV fits PowerGroup cleanly -
a Feb's own name ("SMB_US_2-3") resolves BOTH its DCDCCrates channel
AND its PowerDistStaging feed, matching PowerGroup's "one name resolves
everything" assumption exactly. HV does NOT fit as-is - a group (US/DS
Top/Bottom) has 2 PowerDist channels (HV+weather) and several real HV
channels, none sharing the group's own name, and PowerGroup only
supported ONE power_distribution/hv_supply each. FEB power isn't
modelled by PowerGroup at all (FEBCrates is a 4th equipment kind).

COMMON CHANGE (common/power/group.py): power_distribution/hv_supply
storage changed from a single object to an internal list.
assign_power_distribution(pd)/assign_hv_supply(hv) (singular, auto-
resolve via the group's own id) kept EXACTLY as before - verified
identical behaviour with a standalone smoke test mimicking pixel's own
usage. Added assign_power_distributions(list)/assign_hv_supplies(list)
for pre-resolved multi-channel groups (SciFi's HV case - each channel
resolved by its OWN real name before being handed to the group, since
no single name fits). Added a 4th optional slot, assign_crate_supply()
+ is_crate_on()/turn_on_crate()/turn_off_crate(), for FEB/crate
controller power (duck-type compatible with FebCratePower - same
is_on()/turn_on()/turn_off() names, no inheritance needed). Fixed a
real crash risk: is_on()/read_all_readables() called self.__lv_supply.
is_on()/.read_all() unconditionally (no None-guard) - fine while every
caller always has an LV supply (true for every existing pixel
partition), but SciFi's own HV-only groups have none, so this would
have crashed with AttributeError. Found but did NOT fix (blast radius
on pixel's real JSON configs unknown, could silently change which
partitions get skipped): is_initialized() requires hv_supply is not
None in BOTH its needs_power_distribution branches, contradicting its
own docstring ("Optional - a group can be LV-only") - a real pre-
existing bug, left alone, not called by any of SciFi's own new code.

SCIFI CHANGE (actions/bringup_declaration.py, full rewrite):
- build_lv_power_groups(det): one PowerGroup per Feb (LV + its own
  PowerDist SMB/LDO mains, both via feb.get_lv_partition_name() - new
  getter added). Both assign_lv_supply()/assign_power_distribution()
  return values are checked - a Feb whose name doesn't resolve on
  EITHER equipment is excluded from LV bring-up rather than silently
  using an unresolved channel -1 (a negative-index wraparound bug that
  would have written to the WRONG real channel - caught before it ever
  ran, while building the test's own mock data).
- build_hv_power_groups(det): one PowerGroup per HV group (US/DS Top/
  Bottom), using the new assign_power_distributions()/assign_hv_
  supplies() with pre-resolved channels (hv.ScifiHv.POWERDIST_HV_
  WEATHER_NAMES for PowerDist, hv.group_for_channel() to find each
  group's own real HV channels).
- build_feb_bringup_sequence(det): now ONLY the shared, whole-detector
  prerequisites (TDK "FE Crates", PowerDist FEC crate mains, "Febcrate
  Frontend" program) - the per-Feb part (crate power + link status,
  the actual "fai per feb" ask) is NOT reimplemented here - build_feb_
  crate_sequences(det) returns one common.power.feb_crate.build_feb_
  bringup_sequence() per real Feb, direct reuse of the already-generic,
  pixel-proven mechanism (which itself needed no changes at all).
  scripts/feb_bringup_run.py updated to orchestrate both halves in the
  right order (bring-up: shared first, then per-Feb; power-down:
  per-Feb first, then shared - mirrors BringUpSequence's own reversal
  logic, just applied across two separate sequence objects).
- A real coverage gap caught while writing the new test: the old flat
  LV bring-up also brought up "ch15"/"ch31" (US_LDO/DS_LDO, shared
  per-side infrastructure not owned by any Feb) - the new per-Feb model
  had no owner for them at all. Fixed: a third shared PowerGroup
  (LV_LDO_NAMES) folded into the same "powerdist_smb_ldo_on" step,
  preserving the original step name/behaviour.

VERIFIED: common/power/group.py's backward compatibility checked with a
standalone script exercising pixel's exact single-assignment usage
pattern (unchanged) plus the two new SciFi-shaped scenarios (HV-only
multi-channel group, LV+crate group with no HV) - all pass. Full
scifi_scripts 13-file suite green (tests/test_bringup.py fully
rewritten - new PowerDistStaging mock names for all 12 real Febs'
own SMB channels, not just the 2 previously "commissioned" ones;
new FEB-shared and per-Feb-crate coverage, including a real bring-up
FAIL when the crate controller isn't connected, matching the
detector's actual current assembly state). pixel_scripts' own
test1-6.py all still pass unchanged (none touch PowerGroup directly,
run as a blast-radius check anyway since common/power/group.py was
touched).

ROADMAP POINT 2 - R3 SORTER/MERGER BOTTLENECK DIAGNOSIS, 2026-08-13
====================================================================
Continued the same day. Unlike R1/R2, SwitchingFibre DOES exist in the
local test ODB (it's part of the SciFi-relevant equipment already used
by detector/scifi.py's prepare_switching()/restore_switching()) - so
this piece WAS checked against real live local data, not just the
doc's own cited dump.

REAL STRUCTURE FOUND (via odbedit, not assumed from chain_and_
monitoring.txt's summary text): Settings/Names SFSO (408) is 12 blocks
of 34 fields each ("FEB0".."FEB11" - SB Port + Type + 2 sorter stages
S1/S2 x 16 fields each, including "hits lost in 0/1" and "credits" per
stage). Settings/Names SFCN (234) is 10 global fields + 12 blocks of 18
fields each, and CRITICALLY the first field of each SFCN block is
"FEBn FEB Index" - a real, live-readable field that gives the true
feb_index for that block, rather than trusting block position n to
equal feb_index n. SFSO has no equivalent field of its own - its block
order is trusted to line up with SFCN's (same frontend, same per-FEB
loop), a real residual assumption, documented as such in the code
comment, not hidden.

SCOPE (user, explicit: "2" - fold into the existing per-module rows,
not a separate table) + placement (user, explicit: move the R1/R2
branch-status block from above the whole table to inside it, below the
title/message rows and above the module/link header row - "inserisce
la dentro rdout appena sopra a tabella module link ecc e sotto i
messaggi").

IMPLEMENTED: SciFi_Readout.js gained parseSfcnByFebIndex() (resolves
by the real "FEBn FEB Index" field, not position), parseSfsoByBlock()
(resolves by "FEBn SB Port"/"FEBn Type" block boundaries, sums all
"hits lost in" fields in a block, captures the last "credits" field
seen), buildFebDataFlowStats() (combines both into one febIndex-keyed
map), dataFlowPillFor() (3-state: "data lost"/crit if hits-lost>0 or
LINK FIFO FULL, "flowing"/ok if Merger Rate>0, else "idle"/neutral -
not necessarily wrong, just no throughput to judge, matches today's
idle detector). New "data flow" column added to readout-table (between
"link" and "ASIC active"), colspan bumped 6->7 everywhere in both
SciFi_main.html and SciFi_Readout.js (side-header row, message row,
detail row). R1/R2's branch-status block moved from its own div above
the table into a new <tr> inside it, right after the message row.
VERIFIED (as much as possible without a live browser): replicated the
exact same parsing algorithm in Python against the real dumped Names
SFSO/Names SFCN arrays (not synthetic/invented data) - confirmed
"FEB0 Merger Rate"/"FEB0 LINK FIFO FULL" are found at their real ODB
indices (11, 16) by name, and block boundaries land exactly where the
next "FEBn FEB Index"/"FEBn SB Port" begins. Could not test the actual
rendered page in a browser (no live mhttpd session available this
session) - structure/logic verified, visual rendering not.
Script versions: SciFi_Readout.js v13->v14 (already v11->v13 earlier
same day for R1/R2 + the compact-pill-row correction).

.ROOT/CDB SAVE MECHANISM IMPLEMENTED, 2026-08-13 - the "highest
priority, must work by October" item (section 0) is now closed
====================================================================
After the v2 `.root` design was finalised (see the multi-entry design
block above/`calibration_open_items.txt` section 7) and the 4 blocking
getters were built, user: "vai a vedere gli altri branch" before
starting implementation. Found `common_scripts`/`scifi_scripts`/
`pixel_scripts` were all checked out on feature branches (`gmoretti-*`),
not `main`, and `common_scripts` already had an unrelated, unfinished
`ResultSaver` framework (`common/results/saver.py` + `common/
operation/*.py`, the user's own 2026-08-11 commit, never surfaced in
this session's design work). Confirmed it's dead code in practice for
scifi_scripts (ScanThr/ScanVbr inherit a ResultSaverMultiple via
CalibrationBase but never call append_step_result()/run()/save_final()
- they write ODB by hand). User's explicit call (asked directly):
extend ResultSaver with two new classes rather than build something
bespoke, kept in common_scripts even though only SciFi uses them today
- but without forcing ScanThr/ScanVbr's tick-loop bookkeeping through
the generic step-loop machinery, which doesn't fit and would risk
pixel's own different usage of the same base classes.

Implemented:
- common/results/saver.py: write_root_file(path, {tree_name: rows})
  (row-list -> column-array transpose, uproot.recreate(), empty trees
  skipped). ResultSaverROOT (new). ResultSaverRunDB (was an empty
  stub) - POSTs a temp .root file to <server>/detcal/upload with
  name/run/replace=1 (run number read live from /Runinfo/Run number),
  deletes the temp file after.
- ScanThr.build_root_report()/ScanVbr.build_root_report()
  (calibrations/thr_scan.py, vbr_scan.py) - build the v2 schema dict
  from each scan's own already-tested in-memory state. Supporting
  refactors: entries_covering()/channels_covering() moved from local
  closures to instance methods (report builder reuses the exact same
  per-ASIC covering logic, no second driftable copy); tick-per-
  (offset,threshold) and fault-tick-per-Feb now recorded during
  execute(); start_time/end_time recorded; warnings_for_asic()/
  warnings_for_module() split out and shared between the report and
  the existing ODB write. New calib_base.git_commit_hash() (best-
  effort, try/except None) and calib_base.CDB_SERVER.
- VBR's Conditions deliberately scoped to ONLY the HV channel's own
  status+limits (ScifiHv.get_channel_conditions_all()) - no FEB-level
  fields, since there's no reliable HV-channel-to-Feb mapping today
  (would have been exactly the kind of index-based guess this whole
  roadmap exists to avoid). No ChannelMasks tree for VBR (left open
  per the design doc).
- thr_run.py/vbr_run.py: replaced the "NOTE: .root/CDB result saving
  is not built yet" placeholder with a real save. Local .root save
  always happens; the CDB upload is gated behind a new opt-in
  PySequencer param "save_to_cdb" (default False) since it's a real
  external write to mu3edb0.

New tests/test_root_report.py (write_root_file fixed/jagged/empty-
tree-skip cases, ResultSaverROOT, ResultSaverRunDB with mocked
requests.post - never hits the real mu3edb0 - and both
build_root_report()s against realistic mock scans, round-tripped
through the real uproot writer). All 13 scifi_scripts test scripts
pass. NOT done: a real save_to_cdb=True test against mu3edb0; VBR's
voltage[] is still the commanded value, not a true per-tick readback
(pre-existing, flagged gap); no real end-to-end PySequencer run of
either script has happened yet. Full detail: calibration_open_items.txt
section 7.

====================================================================
START SCAN NOW ACTUALLY LOADS/VERIFIES THE PYSEQUENCER SCRIPT,
2026-08-13
====================================================================
Roadmap "collegare al start scan il PySequencer" (item 1 of 2 Python-
side gaps left on this page - item 2, symlink into userfiles/sequencer,
deferred pending Luigi). Investigated the real mechanism first
(midas/python/midas/sequencer.py + midas/resources/sequencer.js, the
official mhttpd sequencer UI - not guessed): /PySequencer/State/Path +
/State/Filename hold the currently-loaded script; loading a different
one needs writing both plus /PySequencer/Command/Load new file=true,
then polling until that flag self-clears, THEN /Command/Start
script=true. mhttpd's own startSeq() always does load-then-poll-then-
start, never assumes the right script is preloaded.

Found a real, previously-unnoticed gap: SciFi_Calibration.js's
calStartIv()/calStartThr() already wrote /PySequencer/Command/Start
script=true directly, with NO check of what's actually loaded - if the
wrong script (or none) was loaded, this wouldn't have errored, it
would have silently started running whatever WAS loaded. Not what the
user assumed ("mi darà un errore") - actually worse, a silent wrong
action, exactly the class of bug this whole page's design has been
about preventing.

Fixed: new CAL_PYSEQ_SCRIPT_FOR ({ScanVbr: "vbr_run.py", ScanThr:
"thr_run.py"}) + CAL_PYSEQ_SCRIPT_DIR (currently "scifi_scripts" -
placeholder, the real deployed path depends on the still-open symlink
question with Luigi, kept as one easy-to-update constant).
calEnsureScriptAndStart(scriptName, tableId, onStarted): if
calPySeqFilename (already polled live every 2s) already matches,
starts directly; otherwise does the full load+poll+start sequence,
showing a timeout error via the page's own showTableMessage() (not
sequencer.js's dlgAlert()/checkODBValue() - confirmed SciFi_main.html
only loads midas.js+mhttpd.js, not sequencer.js, so those helpers
aren't available here; wrote a page-local equivalent poll instead).
Also fixed a latent ordering issue in the same pass: calStartIv/
calStartThr used to batch ALL writes (calibration params + Start
script) into one modbset() call - modbset() is explicitly documented
(midas.js) as fire-and-forget with no ordering guarantee ACROSS
separate calls, but a SINGLE mjsonrpc_db_paste() call is one RPC
request handled together. Switched both functions from modbset() to
mjsonrpc_db_paste(...).then(...) so the calibration params are
confirmed written before the script-load/start sequence even begins,
preserving the ordering guarantee instead of accidentally relying on
"they happened to be in the same array" once Start script was pulled
out into its own separate step.
SciFi_Calibration.js v46->v47.

NOT done: item 2 (symlink) - waiting on Luigi. Also not done: making
"PySequencer is busy" (calPySeqRunning) an actual HARD block on the
Start button - today it's still only a warning shown alongside
"started", pre-existing behavior, left unchanged since it wasn't part
of what was asked this pass.

====================================================================
.ROOT SAVE: RUN-BOUNDARY + UPSERT-BY-KEY MERGE, 2026-08-13
====================================================================
Real gap found while confirming ResultSaverROOT's overwrite semantics
with the user: the local .root file's name was always "<name>.root",
FIXED, with NO run number in it at all - unlike the CDB path (name+run+
replace=1, real per-run versioning), every single scan save silently
destroyed the PREVIOUS scan's local .root, even across different DAQ
runs. Not "same run overwrites" as assumed - "always overwrites,
regardless of run".

User's proposed fix, then self-connected to a precedent already in
this codebase: file name should include the run number (new file per
run), and for the SAME run, instead of a blind overwrite, do a
key-based diff/merge - "come se fosse meccanismo github" - then
realised out loud this is exactly what ODB array writes already do
(write fccr[5]=123 touches only that cell, nothing else) - just
applied to TTree rows instead of array indices. The one thing ODB
does NOT give for free: run boundaries (ODB only ever has "the latest
value", no run history at all) - that boundary has to be ours.

Implemented in common/results/saver.py:
- ResultSaverROOT.file_name is now computed per save_final() call as
  <name>_<run>.root (file_name_for_run()), not fixed at construction -
  run number comes from an explicit run_number= arg or, if omitted,
  read straight out of the report's own Meta.run.
- New merge_root_file(path, report, row_keys): for each tree with a
  key given in row_keys, reads the existing rows (_read_existing_rows,
  uproot), upserts by key (_upsert_rows - existing rows kept unless a
  new row shares their key, in which case the new value wins; brand-
  new keys appended), then rewrites the whole file. A tree with no key
  given (Meta) is replaced wholesale, matching write_root_file()'s old
  behaviour exactly - a whole-scan summary has no natural row key.
  save_final() only merges if the target file already exists AND
  row_keys were given; otherwise falls back to write_root_file() (first
  save for a run, or a caller that doesn't pass row_keys at all).
- ScanThr.ROOT_ROW_KEYS / ScanVbr.ROOT_ROW_KEYS (calibrations/
  {thr,vbr}_scan.py): the natural per-tree key for each calibration -
  THR: ParametersUsed/folder, RawScan/(folder,offset,threshold),
  Results/(channel,pe_level), Conditions/(feb_index,module_index),
  ChannelMasks/channel. VBR: hv_index for every tree (already the
  common field across ParametersUsed/RawScan/Results/Conditions there).
- thr_run.py/vbr_run.py: save_final() call now passes
  row_keys=scan_thr.ROOT_ROW_KEYS / scan_vbr.ROOT_ROW_KEYS.

Tested (tests/test_root_report.py, new section): two ScanThr saves to
the SAME run number - a RawScan key present in both saves ends up at
the SECOND save's value (update), a key only in the first save (a
threshold range narrowed on the second pass) survives untouched, and a
key only in the second save (a newly-selected second ASIC) is
appended - all in one merged file, confirmed by reading it back with
uproot. All scifi_scripts tests still pass.

PLL TEST - FULL REDESIGN, 2026-08-28 - see pll_test_redesign.txt
====================================================================
Firmware landed 2026-08-28: the `Histogram` tree below was confirmed
wrong (never existed on any real export). Real mechanism found:
`ShiftReport/PLL/<FEB>/<ASIC 0-7>` (histogram) + `SMB_control/{asic,
read_hist,...}` (trigger). Ended up NOT an automated sweep - a manual,
single-step tool (page can't drive a thousands-of-steps loop reliably).
BUILT 2026-08-28: new PLL Test tab moved into Configuration (out of
Calibration), full single-step action wired in SciFi_Configuration.js,
new ODB schema applied on the local test ODB. Verified by replaying the
RPC sequence directly (no browser here) - caught and fixed 2 real bugs
this way (an array-truncation bug, then a silent-failure-on-null-Bins
bug). Full detail in pll_test_redesign.txt.

CORRECTED same day: "how does the firmware know which FEB" turned out
answerable from data already in hand (last_written timestamps across
all 12 FEBs cluster together per ASIC position) - `read_hist`
broadcasts to and writes EVERY FEB at once, there's no FEB target for
running a step. Rebuilt as: one global ASIC+DAC control + one "Run
step (whole detector)" button + a per-FEB result table (click a row to
expand its histogram). No longer an open question for Yfeng.

ASK YFENG MONDAY 2026-08-31, before trusting this tool on the real
system:
1. Legal min/max range for vnvcodelay/vncnt/vnhitlogic (for input
   validation - the write mechanism itself already works either way).
2. What `SMB_control/init_temp`/`stop_temp`/`read_temp` actually do -
   the page has ticks for them but their effect is unknown.

Configuration (built 2026-08-17, see LOGBOOK.md's 2026-08-17 entry for

New tests to add, built on top of the existing Configuration structure:
- PRBS test - `Settings/ASICs/Global/tx_mode = 1` (user's own recollection,
  "mi sembra" - NOT verified against a spec: MuTrig2's tx_mode had a
  documented 2-bit mapping in `Mutrig2Config.cpp` (0=normal, 1=
  prbs_single, 2=prbs_debug, 3=short_event_mode), but the real hardware
  is MuTrig3, whose `Mutrig3Config.cpp` passes tx_mode straight through
  as a raw 3-bit register with no C++-side decode table ("combine flags
  to get more options") - confirmed this repo has no MuTrig3 tx_mode
  bit-meaning documented anywhere (matches `pll_scan.py`'s own tx_mode=4
  for short/PLL mode, a value that doesn't even exist in MuTrig2's
  table). Confirm the real value with Marius/Yfeng before implementing.
- TDC test - no existing procedure found anywhere in `online/` (checked
  `switching_pc/slowcontrol/mutrig/*.cpp/h` thoroughly) - would need to
  be designed from scratch, reusing two existing hardware hooks: the
  `TestPulsesTDC` command (`MutrigFEB::ChangeTDCTest()`, FEB-level test-
  pulse injection toggle, same command `pll_scan.py` already uses) and
  the per-channel `tdctest_n` bit. Exact pass/fail criteria not yet
  discussed - likely something like "does every channel/TDC register
  the injected pulses" (coverage/functionality check), as opposed to
  PLL test's timing-distribution check, but not confirmed with the user.
- PLL test - moves from Calibration into Configuration (Calibration
  keeps only ScanVbr/ScanThr, the calibrations that produce physics
  constants). Loses its ROOT/CDB save path (`build_root_report`,
  `ResultSaverROOT`/`ResultSaverRunDB`) and its ODB `Result/*`
  persistence entirely - becomes a fully live/transient check, verdict
  and histogram computed and shown on demand, nothing written to ODB
  or saved anywhere. User confirmed explicitly ("resta live, non
  persistito").

General controls to add above the FEB table (mirrors the "general
settings above the table, individual info inside the modularity
levels" request):
- The 4 SwitchingFibre commands already used internally by
  `prepare_switching()`/`restore_switching()` in `detector/scifi.py`:
  `Set Bypass`, `Unset Bypass`, `Set FEBs into idle`, `Set FEBs into
  running` - exposed as direct controls so the system can be put into
  bypass and the MuTrigs configured independently of a live run.
- `Reset Counters` (SwitchingFibre) - STILL OPEN, user hasn't decided
  ("non lo so ancora"). Found while auditing the full command list:
  it's generic, not Mupix-prefixed like most of SwitchingFibre's other
  ~45 commands (those are pixel_scripts territory, not ours) - likely
  resets the FCC* count variables below too, which is why it came up.

  confirmed to be exactly the 3 already known/used: `MutrigConfig`,
  `MutrigConfigAllOff`, `TestPulsesTDC` - nothing missing.

  Configuration today; the other 5 (`FCCH`, `FCCF`, `FCCE`, `FCLS`,
  `FCCP`, confirmed present in the real ODB export checked the same

  thr_scan.py, used by scripts/vbr_run.py and thr_run.py) - it
  upserts ROOT tree rows by key into the existing per-run .root file.
  ResultSaverCDB (both the merged main version and the old, stale,
  pre-reorg lvigani task/CDB branch - c74d862, never merged, based on
  an old core.detector/core.daq pixel-only structure) has NO such
  check - save_final()/upload_file() just POSTs with replace=True
  unconditionally, no comparison against what's already on CDB.
  User's intended new design (confirmed correct): every calibration
  always saves locally (already true), then a SEPARATE, later,
  curated step selects which of the local saves actually gets pushed
  to CDB - CDB upload should NOT be automatic/coupled to the
  calibration run itself. Still not designed: what does the
  selection step - a standalone script or a page feature (page-before-
  code default applies once this gets picked up for real).

  one - not touched, out of scope for this pass).
  Caught and fixed one real bug of my own along the way: first attempt
  wired configure_alloff as an ODBBooleanCommand (mirroring pixel's
  configure pattern) - but the ORIGINAL configure_alloff() was always a
  plain fire-and-forget ODBCommand WRITE, no wait-for-revert behaviour.
  ODBBooleanCommand waits up to 30s for its key to read back something
  other than the written value (simulates real hardware auto-resetting
  the boolean) - MutrigConfigAllOff has no such auto-reset (unlike
  MutrigConfig, which test_mock_odb_data.py explicitly flips back for),
  so this made every test constructing a SciFiDetector and calling this
  path spin for 30s. Reverted to the original plain-write behaviour.
  Updated common_scripts/tests/test_daq.py (was testing PixelDAQControl/
  PixelDAQLVDSErrorStats directly, no longer in common) to exercise

  suite (e.g. test_partition.py's FakeClient).

  work test_actionfactory.py does - not a regression), common_scripts
  11/11, pixel 5/6 (test4's known pre-existing bug only). All 3 repos

     existing dry-run mock data.

     used common/operation/operation_factory.py's OperationFactoryBase

     actionfactory.py's ActionFactory, which already reuses it via a
     lambda adapter. Root cause: OperationFactoryBase's built-in
     shared-equipment check only supports one name_filter, but SciFi's
     own curated check needs several. Fixed properly instead of just
     correcting the (previously wrong) comment claiming they were
     already shared: added an optional equipment_check callable param to

     every existing caller) and rewired CalibrationFactory to pass

     calibration lambda adapters (ScanThr(det) etc. take 1 arg, dispatch()
     always calls operation_class(det, daq_control)) - same adapter
     pattern as the detector_class one already used for actions.
